Units of Measurement in Building

Reference table of SI, statutory, and older units used in building measurement, including symbols and conversion factors. The text states that the international system of measurement with SI units has been valid since 1 January 1978.

PDF 17 · Book 4

Paper Formats

Standardized paper formats provide a basis for office furniture design and floor-plan development. The A series is formed by halving or doubling the basic format; B and C formats serve dependent paper sizes. Strip formats are lengthwise divisions of main formats. The page also gives A4 layout and type-area measurements, and discusses the sizing of binders, pads, and books.

The formats in the C series are the geometric mean\ndimensions of the A and B series ~ 0.\n\n8 Sheet sizes\n\nStrip (or side margin) formats are made by dividing the main formats\nlengthwise into halves, quarters and eighths (for envelopes, signs,\ndrawings etc.) ~ 0 + e.\n\nFormat\n\nAbbreviation\n\nmm\n\nhalf length A4\n\n%A4\n\n105 X 297\n\nquarter length A4\n\nY.A4\n\n52 X 297\n\neighth length A7\n\nY.A7\n\n9x 105\n\nhalf length C4\n\nY, C4\n\n114 X 324\n\nFile cards without tabs correspond exactly to the standard\nformats. Tab cards are larger to allow for the tab, i.e. they have a\nprojection at the upper edge for classification.\nBinders, files and folders are wider than the standard format\nto provide space for the fixing mechanism. Widths should if\npossible be selected from the possible dimensions from series\n\netc.\n\ne\n\nPaper formats have generally been standardised (apart from\nin the USA) to conform to the internationally accepted {ISO)\nseries of paper sheet sizes {A,B,C,D). These were developed\non the basis of an area of 1 m2 , divided according to the ratio\nof the sides:\n\nStrip formats\n\nA, s, c~e.\n\nA4\n\n(it Strip formats in A4\n\n0 Loose-leaf binder\nr - - - - - 210 -----!\n\nPads and carbonless duplicate books have precisely the standard\nformats; if there is a standing perforated edge, then here the\nsheets are smaller than the standard format~ e.\nBound and trimmed books have precisely the standard format.\nIf a further trim is necessary during binding, then the pages will\nbe slightly smaller than the standard format, and the cover will\nproject accordingly. The cover size must be at least the standard\nformat ~ (). The formats in the C series are the geometric mean\ndimensions of the A and B series ~ 0.\n\n8 Sheet sizes\n\nStrip (or side margin) formats are made by dividing the main formats\nlengthwise into halves, quarters and eighths (for envelopes, signs,\ndrawings etc.) ~ 0 + e.\n\nFormat\n\nAbbreviation\n\nmm\n\nhalf length A4\n\n%A4\n\n105 X 297\n\nquarter length A4\n\nY.A4\n\n52 X 297\n\neighth length A7\n\nY.A7\n\n9x 105\n\nhalf length C4\n\nY, C4\n\n114 X 324\n\nFile cards without tabs correspond exactly to the standard\nformats. Tab cards are larger to allow for the tab, i.e. they have a\nprojection at the upper edge for classification.\nBinders, files and folders are wider than the standard format\nto provide space for the fixing mechanism. Widths should if\npossible be selected from the possible dimensions from series\n\netc.\n\ne\n\nPaper formats have generally been standardised (apart from\nin the USA) to conform to the internationally accepted {ISO)\nseries of paper sheet sizes {A,B,C,D). These were developed\non the basis of an area of 1 m2 , divided according to the ratio\nof the sides:\n\nStrip formats\n\nA, s, c~e.\n\nA4\n\n(it Strip formats in A4\n\n0 Loose-leaf binder\nr - - - - - 210 -----!\n\nPads and carbonless duplicate books have precisely the standard\nformats; if there is a standing perforated edge, then here the\nsheets are smaller than the standard format~ e.\nBound and trimmed books have precisely the standard format.\nIf a further trim is necessary during binding, then the pages will\nbe slightly smaller than the standard format, and the cover will\nproject accordingly. The cover size must be at least the standard\nformat ~ (). The formats in the C series are the geometric mean\ndimensions of the A and B series ~ 0.\n\n8 Sheet sizes\n\nStrip (or side margin) formats are made by dividing the main formats\nlengthwise into halves, quarters and eighths (for envelopes, signs,\ndrawings etc.) ~ 0 + e.\n\nFormat\n\nAbbreviation\n\nmm\n\nhalf length A4\n\n%A4\n\n105 X 297\n\nquarter length A4\n\nY.A4\n\n52 X 297\n\neighth length A7\n\nY.A7\n\n9x 105\n\nhalf length C4\n\nY, C4\n\n114 X 324\n\nFile cards without tabs correspond exactly to the standard\nformats. Tab cards are larger to allow for the tab, i.e. they have a\nprojection at the upper edge for classification.\nBinders, files and folders are wider than the standard format\nto provide space for the fixing mechanism. Widths should if\npossible be selected from the possible dimensions from series\n\netc.\n\ne\n\nPaper formats have generally been standardised (apart from\nin the USA) to conform to the internationally accepted {ISO)\nseries of paper sheet sizes {A,B,C,D). These were developed\non the basis of an area of 1 m2 , divided according to the ratio\nof the sides:\n\nStrip formats\n\nA, s, c~e.\n\nA4\n\n(it Strip formats in A4\n\n0 Loose-leaf binder\nr - - - - - 210 -----!\n\nPads and carbonless duplicate books have precisely the standard\nformats; if there is a standing perforated edge, then here the\nsheets are smaller than the standard format~ e.\nBound and trimmed books have precisely the standard format.\nIf a further trim is necessary during binding, then the pages will\nbe slightly smaller than the standard format, and the cover will\nproject accordingly. The cover size must be at least the standard\nformat ~ (). The cover width is determined by the binding\nprocess.\npicas\n\nfj) Pads, carbonless duplicate books\n\nFoot border\n\nC) Bound and trimmed books\n\n4\n\nmm\n\ntype area width\n\n39.51\n\n40.5\n\n167\n\n171\n\ntype area height (without header/footer)\n\n58.5\n\n59\n\n247\n\n250\n\n1\n\nspace between columns\n\n1\n\nmax. width, double columns\n\n39.5\n\n167\n\nmax. width, single column\n\n19\n\n81\n\n5\n\ninside (gutter) margin, nominal\n\n16\n\n14\n\nouter (side) margin, nominal\n\n27\n\n25\n\ntop (head) margin, nominal\n\n20\n\n19\n\nbottom (foot) margin, nominal\n\n30\n\n28\n\n~ Layouts and type area of the A4 standard format -> C!)\n\n\f"

The formats in the C series are the geometric mean\ndimensions of the A and B series ~ 0.\n\n8 Sheet sizes\n\nStrip (or side margin) formats are made by dividing the main formats\nlengthwise into halves, quarters and eighths (for envelopes, signs,\ndrawings etc.) ~ 0 + e.\n\nFormat\n\nAbbreviation\n\nmm\n\nhalf length A4\n\n%A4\n\n105 X 297\n\nquarter length A4\n\nY.A4\n\n52 X 297\n\neighth length A7\n\nY.A7\n\n9x 105\n\nhalf length C4\n\nY, C4\n\n114 X 324\n\nFile cards without tabs correspond exactly to the standard\nformats. Tab cards are larger to allow for the tab, i.e. they have a\nprojection at the upper edge for classification.\nBinders, files and folders are wider than the standard format\nto provide space for the fixing mechanism. Widths should if\npossible be selected from the possible dimensions from series\n\netc.\n\ne\n\nPaper formats have generally been standardised (apart from\nin the USA) to conform to the internationally accepted {ISO)\nseries of paper sheet sizes {A,B,C,D). These were developed\non the basis of an area of 1 m2 , divided according to the ratio\nof the sides:\n\nStrip formats\n\nA, s, c~e.\n\nA4\n\n(it Strip formats in A4\n\n0 Loose-leaf binder\nr - - - - - 210 -----!\n\nPads and carbonless duplicate books have precisely the standard\nformats; if there is a standing perforated edge, then here the\nsheets are smaller than the standard format~ e.\nBound and trimmed books have precisely the standard format.\nIf a further trim is necessary during binding, then the pages will\nbe slightly smaller than the standard format, and the cover will\nproject accordingly. The cover size must be at least the standard\nformat ~ (). The cover width is determined by the binding\nprocess.\npicas\n\nfj) Pads, carbonless duplicate books\n\nFoot border\n\nC) Bound and trimmed books\n\n4\n\nmm\n\ntype area width\n\n39.51\n\n40.5\n\n167\n\n171\n\ntype area height (without header/footer)\n\n58.5\n\n59\n\n247\n\n250\n\n1\n\nspace between columns\n\n1\n\nmax. width, double columns\n\n39.5\n\n167\n\nmax. width, single column\n\n19\n\n81\n\n5\n\ninside (gutter) margin, nominal\n\n16\n\n14\n\nouter (side) margin, nominal\n\n27\n\n25\n\ntop (head) margin, nominal\n\n20\n\n19\n\nbottom (foot) margin, nominal\n\n30\n\n28\n\n~ Layouts and type area of the A4 standard format -> C!)\n\n\f"

The formats in the C series are the geometric mean\ndimensions of the A and B series ~ 0.\n\n8 Sheet sizes\n\nStrip (or side margin) formats are made by dividing the main formats\nlengthwise into halves, quarters and eighths (for envelopes, signs,\ndrawings etc.) ~ 0 + e.\n\nFormat\n\nAbbreviation\n\nmm\n\nhalf length A4\n\n%A4\n\n105 X 297\n\nquarter length A4\n\nY.A4\n\n52 X 297\n\neighth length A7\n\nY.A7\n\n9x 105\n\nhalf length C4\n\nY, C4\n\n114 X 324\n\nFile cards without tabs correspond exactly to the standard\nformats. Tab cards are larger to allow for the tab, i.e. they have a\nprojection at the upper edge for classification.\nBinders, files and folders are wider than the standard format\nto provide space for the fixing mechanism. Widths should if\npossible be selected from the possible dimensions from series\n\netc.\n\ne\n\nPaper formats have generally been standardised (apart from\nin the USA) to conform to the internationally accepted {ISO)\nseries of paper sheet sizes {A,B,C,D). These were developed\non the basis of an area of 1 m2 , divided according to the ratio\nof the sides:\n\nStrip formats\n\nA, s, c~e.\n\nA4\n\n(it Strip formats in A4\n\n0 Loose-leaf binder\nr - - - - - 210 -----!\n\nPads and carbonless duplicate books have precisely the standard\nformats; if there is a standing perforated edge, then here the\nsheets are smaller than the standard format~ e.\nBound and trimmed books have precisely the standard format.\nIf a further trim is necessary during binding, then the pages will\nbe slightly smaller than the standard format, and the cover will\nproject accordingly. The cover size must be at least the standard\nformat ~ (). The formats in the C series are the geometric mean\ndimensions of the A and B series ~ 0.\n\n8 Sheet sizes\n\nStrip (or side margin) formats are made by dividing the main formats\nlengthwise into halves, quarters and eighths (for envelopes, signs,\ndrawings etc.) ~ 0 + e.\n\nFormat\n\nAbbreviation\n\nmm\n\nhalf length A4\n\n%A4\n\n105 X 297\n\nquarter length A4\n\nY.A4\n\n52 X 297\n\neighth length A7\n\nY.A7\n\n9x 105\n\nhalf length C4\n\nY, C4\n\n114 X 324\n\nFile cards without tabs correspond exactly to the standard\nformats. Tab cards are larger to allow for the tab, i.e. they have a\nprojection at the upper edge for classification.\nBinders, files and folders are wider than the standard format\nto provide space for the fixing mechanism. Widths should if\npossible be selected from the possible dimensions from series\n\netc.\n\ne\n\nPaper formats have generally been standardised (apart from\nin the USA) to conform to the internationally accepted {ISO)\nseries of paper sheet sizes {A,B,C,D). The cover size must be at least the standard\nformat ~ (). The formats in the C series are the geometric mean\ndimensions of the A and B series ~ 0.\n\n8 Sheet sizes\n\nStrip (or side margin) formats are made by dividing the main formats\nlengthwise into halves, quarters and eighths (for envelopes, signs,\ndrawings etc.) ~ 0 + e.\n\nFormat\n\nAbbreviation\n\nmm\n\nhalf length A4\n\n%A4\n\n105 X 297\n\nquarter length A4\n\nY.A4\n\n52 X 297\n\neighth length A7\n\nY.A7\n\n9x 105\n\nhalf length C4\n\nY, C4\n\n114 X 324\n\nFile cards without tabs correspond exactly to the standard\nformats. Tab cards are larger to allow for the tab, i.e. they have a\nprojection at the upper edge for classification.\nBinders, files and folders are wider than the standard format\nto provide space for the fixing mechanism. Widths should if\npossible be selected from the possible dimensions from series\n\netc.\n\ne\n\nPaper formats have generally been standardised (apart from\nin the USA) to conform to the internationally accepted {ISO)\nseries of paper sheet sizes {A,B,C,D). These were developed\non the basis of an area of 1 m2 , divided according to the ratio\nof the sides:\n\nStrip formats\n\nA, s, c~e.\n\nA4\n\n(it Strip formats in A4\n\n0 Loose-leaf binder\nr - - - - - 210 -----!\n\nPads and carbonless duplicate books have precisely the standard\nformats; if there is a standing perforated edge, then here the\nsheets are smaller than the standard format~ e.\nBound and trimmed books have precisely the standard format.\nIf a further trim is necessary during binding, then the pages will\nbe slightly smaller than the standard format, and the cover will\nproject accordingly. The cover size must be at least the standard\nformat ~ (). The formats in the C series are the geometric mean\ndimensions of the A and B series ~ 0.\n\n8 Sheet sizes\n\nStrip (or side margin) formats are made by dividing the main formats\nlengthwise into halves, quarters and eighths (for envelopes, signs,\ndrawings etc.) ~ 0 + e.\n\nFormat\n\nAbbreviation\n\nmm\n\nhalf length A4\n\n%A4\n\n105 X 297\n\nquarter length A4\n\nY.A4\n\n52 X 297\n\neighth length A7\n\nY.A7\n\n9x 105\n\nhalf length C4\n\nY, C4\n\n114 X 324\n\nFile cards without tabs correspond exactly to the standard\nformats. PDF 18 · Book 5

Technical Drawings: Standard Sheet Formats and Folding

Explains the use of ISO A-series drawing sheets, title-block placement, paper-roll widths, and folding drawings for A4 box-file storage. Standard formats support layout, discussion, posting, and filing; folding instructions keep the title block visible and identify the punched or clamped edge.

At the start of folding, a width of 21 em must always be folded\nfirst (fold 1), ideally with the use of a 21 x 29.7 em template.\n3. A triangle is folded into the drawing starting from c (fold 2) so\nthat on the completely folded drawing only the below left field\nmarked with a cross is punched or clamped.\n4. The resulting strip is folded from side b.\n\n~\n\nI 0\n\n.1\n\"'\"i\u2022 l box\n\n0 ISOsizeA4\n\ne\n\nFrom normal roll widths, the following sizes can be used to provide\nformats in the A series:\nfor drawing paper, tracing paper\n1500, 1560 mm\n(derived from these:\n250, 1250, 660, 900 mm)\nfor print paper:\n650, 900, 1200 mm.\nIf all the drawing formats up to AO are to be cut from a paper web,\na roll width of at least 900 mm will be necessary.\nDrawings which are to be stored in A4 box files should be folded\nas follows --> 0:\n\nA piece of card of size A5 = 14.8 x 21 em can be glued to the back\nof the punched side to reinforce the edge. Any sheet size can be\nfolded by following the instructions above. If the drawing length\nremaining after the folding of the first 21 em cannot be divided by\n18.5 em into an even number 2, 4, 6, etc., then the remaining width\nshould be folded in the centre .\n\n0 ISOsizeA3\n'\n\nThe title block should be the following distance from the edge of\nthe drawing:\n10 mm\nfor formats AO-A3\nfor formats A4-A6\n5 mm\nFor small drawings, a filing margin of up to 25 mm can be used,\nwhich reduces the usable area of the finished format. As an\nexception, narrow formats can be made by adding together a\nrow of identical or adjacent shapes from the format range.\n\nI\nI\n\n~\n\ncut-out ISO A4\n\nISOsizeA5\n\nNo. identical fields by sheet size\n\nDivision for\nAO\na\nb\n\n16\n12\n\nI A1 I A2 I A3 I A4\n\nI 1~ I ~ I ~ I\n\n0 Field divisions (grid squares)\n\nISOAO\n\n4\n4\n\nISOA1\n\nISOA2\n\nFolding schemes and dimensions\n\n5\n\nDRAWINGS\nPaper formats\nTechnical\ndrawings\nLayout of\ndrawings\nConstruction\ndrawings\nConstruction\ndrawing symbols\nWater supply and\ndrainage symbols\nElectrical\ninstallation\nsymbols\nSecurity\ninstallalion\nsymbols\nGas installation\nsymbols\nDrawing by hand\nComputer-aided\ndrawing\nBS EN ISO 128\nBS 1192\nBS EN ISO 4157\nDIN 824\n\n\f"

The title block must always be at the front, in the correct\nposition and clearly visible.\n2. At the start of folding, a width of 21 em must always be folded\nfirst (fold 1), ideally with the use of a 21 x 29.7 em template.\n3. A triangle is folded into the drawing starting from c (fold 2) so\nthat on the completely folded drawing only the below left field\nmarked with a cross is punched or clamped.\n4. The drawing is then folded parallel to side a to a width of\n18.5 em, for which a template of 18.5 x 29.8 em is useful.\nThe last section is folded in half to adjust the sheet size,\nbringing the title block to the front. Long narrow formats can be\ncorrespondingly folded.\n5. The resulting strip is folded from side b.\n\n~\n\nI 0\n\n.1\n\"'\"i\u2022 l box\n\n0 ISOsizeA4\n\ne\n\nFrom normal roll widths, the following sizes can be used to provide\nformats in the A series:\nfor drawing paper, tracing paper\n1500, 1560 mm\n(derived from these:\n250, 1250, 660, 900 mm)\nfor print paper:\n650, 900, 1200 mm.\nIf all the drawing formats up to AO are to be cut from a paper web,\na roll width of at least 900 mm will be necessary.\nDrawings which are to be stored in A4 box files should be folded\nas follows --> 0:\n\nA piece of card of size A5 = 14.8 x 21 em can be glued to the back\nof the punched side to reinforce the edge. Any sheet size can be\nfolded by following the instructions above. If the drawing length\nremaining after the folding of the first 21 em cannot be divided by\n18.5 em into an even number 2, 4, 6, etc., then the remaining width\nshould be folded in the centre .\n\n0 ISOsizeA3\n'\n\nThe title block should be the following distance from the edge of\nthe drawing:\n10 mm\nfor formats AO-A3\nfor formats A4-A6\n5 mm\nFor small drawings, a filing margin of up to 25 mm can be used,\nwhich reduces the usable area of the finished format. As an\nexception, narrow formats can be made by adding together a\nrow of identical or adjacent shapes from the format range.\n\nI\nI\n\n~\n\ncut-out ISO A4\n\nISOsizeA5\n\nNo. identical fields by sheet size\n\nDivision for\nAO\na\nb\n\n16\n12\n\nI A1 I A2 I A3 I A4\n\nI 1~ I ~ I ~ I\n\n0 Field divisions (grid squares)\n\nISOAO\n\n4\n4\n\nISOA1\n\nISOA2\n\nFolding schemes and dimensions\n\n5\n\nDRAWINGS\nPaper formats\nTechnical\ndrawings\nLayout of\ndrawings\nConstruction\ndrawings\nConstruction\ndrawing symbols\nWater supply and\ndrainage symbols\nElectrical\ninstallation\nsymbols\nSecurity\ninstallalion\nsymbols\nGas installation\nsymbols\nDrawing by hand\nComputer-aided\ndrawing\nBS EN ISO 128\nBS 1192\nBS EN ISO 4157\nDIN 824\n\n\f"

Any sheet size can be\nfolded by following the instructions above. If the drawing length\nremaining after the folding of the first 21 em cannot be divided by\n18.5 em into an even number 2, 4, 6, etc., then the remaining width\nshould be folded in the centre .\n\n0 ISOsizeA3\n'\n\nThe title block should be the following distance from the edge of\nthe drawing:\n10 mm\nfor formats AO-A3\nfor formats A4-A6\n5 mm\nFor small drawings, a filing margin of up to 25 mm can be used,\nwhich reduces the usable area of the finished format. As an\nexception, narrow formats can be made by adding together a\nrow of identical or adjacent shapes from the format range.\n\nI\nI\n\n~\n\ncut-out ISO A4\n\nISOsizeA5\n\nNo. identical fields by sheet size\n\nDivision for\nAO\na\nb\n\n16\n12\n\nI A1 I A2 I A3 I A4\n\nI 1~ I ~ I ~ I\n\n0 Field divisions (grid squares)\n\nISOAO\n\n4\n4\n\nISOA1\n\nISOA2\n\nFolding schemes and dimensions\n\n5\n\nDRAWINGS\nPaper formats\nTechnical\ndrawings\nLayout of\ndrawings\nConstruction\ndrawings\nConstruction\ndrawing symbols\nWater supply and\ndrainage symbols\nElectrical\ninstallation\nsymbols\nSecurity\ninstallalion\nsymbols\nGas installation\nsymbols\nDrawing by hand\nComputer-aided\ndrawing\nBS EN ISO 128\nBS 1192\nBS EN ISO 4157\nDIN 824\n\n\f"

A triangle is folded into the drawing starting from c (fold 2) so\nthat on the completely folded drawing only the below left field\nmarked with a cross is punched or clamped.\n4.
